=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is a grid of H rows and W columns.
The rows are numbered 0,1,\ldots,H-1 from top to bottom, and the columns are numbered 0,1,\ldots,W-1 from left to right.
Let (i,j) denote the cell at row i and column j.
You are given H strings S_0, S_1, \ldots, S_{H-1}, each of which is of length W and consists of A and B.
In each cell, one of the following two types of tiles is placed.
Let S_{ij} denote the (j+1)-th character (0 \le j \le W-1) of the string S_i. The type of tile placed in cell (i,j) is S_{ij}.

- Type A: A single line segment is drawn on the tile’s surface, connecting the midpoints of two adjacent edges.



- Type B: A single line segment is drawn on the tile’s surface, connecting the midpoints of two opposite edges.


These tiles can be freely rotated. When focusing only on the pattern formed by the line segments, there are four ways to rotate a Type-A tile and two ways to rotate a Type-B tile. Therefore, if we distinguish placements only by the pattern of line segments, the number of ways to place the tiles is 4^a \times 2^b, where a is the number of Type-A tiles and b is the number of Type-B tiles.
Among these ways, print the number, modulo 998244353, of ways such that the line segments on the tiles have no dead ends when viewing the grid as a torus.
Here, "the line segments on the tiles have no dead ends when viewing the grid as a torus" if and only if the following two conditions are satisfied for every cell (i,j):

- Both of the following exist, or neither of the following exists:
- the line segment drawn in the cell (i,j), whose endpoint is the midpoint of the right edge of the cell (i,j)
- the line segment drawn in the cell (i,(j+1)\bmod W), whose endpoint is the midpoint of the left edge of the cell (i,(j+1)\bmod W)


- Both of the following exist, or neither of the following exists:
- the line segment drawn in the cell (i,j), whose endpoint is the midpoint of the bottom edge of the cell (i,j)
- the line segment drawn in the cell ((i+1)\bmod H,j), whose endpoint is the midpoint of the top edge of the cell ((i+1)\bmod H,j)



For example, the following placement satisfies the condition:

The following placement does not satisfy the condition. Specifically, while there is no line segment whose endpoint is the midpoint of the right edge of the tile in cell (0,2), there is a line segment whose endpoint is the midpoint of the left edge of the tile in cell (0,0), so the condition is not satisfied.

You are given T test cases; solve each of them.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
T
case_1
case_2
\vdots
case_T

Each case is given in the following format:
H W
S_0
S_1
\vdots
S_{H-1}

Output

For each test case, print the number, modulo 998244353, of placements that satisfies the condition, in separate lines.

Constraints


- 1 \le T \le 10^5
- 2 \le H,W
- HW\leq 10^6
- S_i\,(0\le i\le H-1) are length-W strings consisting of A and B.
- The sum of H W over all test cases is at most 10^6.
- T, H, and W are integers.

Sample Input 1

3
3 3
AAB
AAB
BBB
3 3
BBA
ABA
AAB
3 4
BAAB
BABA
BBAA

Sample Output 1

2
0
2

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

def find(x):

Alternative DSU parity without recursion:
def find(x):
    if parent[x] != x:
        root, px = find(parent[x])
        xor_to_parent[x] ^= px
        parent[x] = root
    return parent[x], xor_to_parent[x]
Here px is xor from parent to root. Initially xor_to_parent[x] = value[x] xor value[parent[x]]. After parent[x] becomes root, xor_to_parent[x] should become value[x] xor value[root]. It equals old xor_to_parent[x] xor (value[parent] xor value[root]) = xor_to_parent[x] ^ px. Good.

Union(u,v,w): relation value[u] xor value[v] = w.

If attach ru under rv: parent[ru]=rv. Need set xor_to_parent[ru] = value[ru] xor value[rv]. We know value[u] xor value[v] = w.

value[v] = xv xor value[rv]
So (xu xor value[ru]) xor (xv xor value[rv]) = w

Thus xor_to_parent[ru] = value[ru] xor value[rv] = w ^ xu ^ xv. If attach ru to rv. If attach rv to ru, need value[rv] xor value[ru] = same (since xor symmetric), so xor_to_parent[rv] = same too? Need ensure formula for K and DSU graph correct for all cases. Let's derive more rigorously.

Original variables:
For each cell (i,j):
Let L = x_{i,j-1}, R=x_{i,j}, U=y_{i-1,j}, D=y_{i,j}.
Type A: L⊕R=1, U⊕D=1.
Type B: L=R, U=D, L⊕U=1.

Define a=1 for A, 0 for B.
Then for all cells:
R = L ⊕ a. (since A flip, B copy)
D = U ⊕ a. (since A flip, B copy)
For B (a=0): L⊕U=1.

Now solve R=L⊕a around row. Let r_i = x_{i,0}. Then for any j:
x_{i,j} = r_i ⊕ P_i(j), where P_i(0)=0, P_i(j)=⊕_{k=1}^j a_{i,k} for 1<=j<=W-1, and for left edge of j=0, x_{i,-1}=x_{i,W-1}=r_i⊕P_i(W-1). The equation for j=0: x_{i,0}=x_{i,W-1}⊕a_{i,0} => r_i = r_i⊕P_i(W-1)⊕a_{i,0} => P_i(W-1)=a_{i,0}. This is row parity condition. So for valid, P_i(W-1)=a_{i,0}. Thus P_i(left) for B at j=0 is a_{i,0}. Our row_pref[0] computed as xor_{k=1}^{W-1} a_k; if valid equals a0. Good.

Similarly y: let c_j = y_{0,j}. y_{i,j}=c_j⊕Q_j(i), Q_j(0)=0, Q_j(i)=⊕_{k=1}^i a_{k,j}. For top edge of i=0, y_{-1,j}=y_{H-1,j}=c_j⊕Q_j(H-1), and equation i=0: y_{0,j}=y_{H-1,j}⊕a_{0,j} => c_j=c_j⊕Q_j(H-1)⊕a0 => Q_j(H-1)=a0. Column parity. Thus for B at i=0, Q_top = Q_j(H-1)=a0. For i>0, Q_top=Q_j(i-1)=⊕_{k=1}^{i-1} a_{k,j}. Our col_pref for i>0 is exactly that. Good.

Then B equation at cell (i,j): L⊕U =1.
L = x_{i,j-1} = r_i ⊕ P_i(left) (with P_i(left) as above).
U = y_{i-1,j} = c_j ⊕ Q_j(top).
So r_i⊕c_j = 1⊕P_i(left)⊕Q_j(top)=K. Good.

Parity DSU iterative find:
def find(x):
    root = x
    acc = 0
    while parent[root] != root:
        acc ^= xor_to_parent[root]
        root = parent[root]
    # compress path
    while parent[x] != x:
        p = parent[x]
        px = xor_to_parent[x]
        parent[x] = root
        xor_to_parent[x] = acc  # Wait acc from x to root? Need compute per node.
        acc ^= px? Let's derive.

Simpler recursive is fine. But recursive find with path compression and union by size depth O(log n) <= ~20, so recursion depth small. Actually union by size ensures depth <= log2 n < 20 for n<=5e5. So recursionlimit not issue. But if we don't union by size? We will. Recursive find depth small. Use sys.setrecursionlimit(1_000_000) anyway.

Pseudo:

MOD = 998244353

def solve_case(H,W,grid):
    # grid list of bytes
    col_par = [0]*W
    row_par = [0]*H
    for i,s in enumerate(grid):
        p=0
        # maybe use s.count(b'A')? bytes.count expects sub bytes, returns count. Since only A/B, row_par = count_A &1. col_par needs per char.
        # For speed, loop over enumerate(s)
        for j,ch in enumerate(s):
            if ch == 65:
                p ^= 1
                col_par[j] ^= 1
        row_par[i]=p
    if any(row_par) or any(col_par): return 0

    n = H + W
    parent = list(range(n))
    size = [1]*n
    xor_to_parent = [0]*n
    comps = n
    ok = True

    def find(x):
        if parent[x] != x:
            r, px = find(parent[x])
            xor_to_parent[x] ^= px
            parent[x] = r
        return parent[x], xor_to_parent[x]

    def unite(u,v,w):
        nonlocal comps, ok
        ru, xu = find(u)
        rv, xv = find(v)
        if ru == rv:
            if (xu ^ xv) != w:
                ok = False
            return
        # attach smaller to larger
        if size[ru] < size[rv]:
            parent[ru] = rv
            xor_to_parent[ru] = w ^ xu ^ xv
            size[rv] += size[ru]
        else:
            parent[rv] = ru
            xor_to_parent[rv] = w ^ xu ^ xv  # same? Wait if attach rv under ru, need xor_to_parent[rv] = value[rv] xor value[ru]. We computed value[ru] xor value[rv] = w ^ xu ^ xv. Since xor symmetric, value[rv] xor value[ru] = same. So yes.
            size[ru] += size[rv]
        comps -= 1

But wait if attach ru under rv, xor_to_parent[ru] = value[ru] xor value[rv] = w ^ xu ^ xv. If attach rv under ru, xor_to_parent[rv] = value[rv] xor value[ru] = w ^ xu ^ xv. Since GF2, same. Good.

    row_pref = [0]*W
    pref = 0
    # compute row_pref for j=1..W-1
    # Need a[1..W-1] ints. Since s is bytes, ch==65.
    for j in range(1, W):

        if s[j] == 65:
            pref ^= 1

    # Now iterate j=0..W-1:
    for j,ch in enumerate(s):
        if ch == 66: # B

            if i == 0:
                Q = 1 if s[j] == 65? Wait row0 Q = a_{0,j} (if col parity valid). But if i==0, Q = a0 = 1 if s[j]=='A' else 0.
            else:
                Q = col_pref[j]  # xor_{k=1}^{i-1} a[k][j]
            K = 1 ^ P ^ Q
            unite(i, H+j, K)
            if not ok: maybe return early
    # After processing row i, update col_pref for next row if i>=1? Need maintain col_pref.
But col_pref used for row i>0. We need initialize col_pref = [0]*W before row loop. For row0, Q=a0, not col_pref. After row0, col_pref remains 0. For row1, Q=col_pref=0. After processing row1, update col_pref ^= row1. For row2, Q=row1. So in loop:
    col_pref = [0]*W
    for i in range(H):
        if i == 0:
            # Q uses a0; no col_pref update
        else:
            # Q uses col_pref
        process row
        if i >= 1:
            # update col_pref with row i for future

But wait for i=1, after processing row1, update col_pref ^= row1. For i=2, Q=row1. Good. For i=2, after processing update col_pref ^= row2; for i=3, Q=row1^row2 = xor_{k=1}^{2} a_k. Formula for i=3 top edge y_{2,j}: Q_j(2)=xor_{k=1}^{2} a_k. Yes. So update after each row i>=1. What about row0? We do not update, so before row1 col_pref=0. Good.

import sys
MOD=998244353
def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    it = iter(data)
    T = int(next(it))
    out=[]
    for _ in range(T):
        H = int(next(it)); W = int(next(it))
        grid = [next(it).strip() for _ in range(H)]  # bytes
        # first pass
        col_par = [0]*W
        row_par = [0]*H
        for i,s in enumerate(grid):
            p=0
            # loop
            for j,ch in enumerate(s):
                if ch == 65: # A
                    p ^= 1
                    col_par[j] ^= 1
            row_par[i]=p
        if any(row_par) or any(col_par):
            out.append("0")
            continue
        # DSU
        n = H + W
        parent = list(range(n))
        size = [1]*n
        xr = [0]*n
        comps = n
        ok = True

        sys.setrecursionlimit(1_000_000)
        def find(x):
            if parent[x] != x:
                r, px = find(parent[x])
                xr[x] ^= px
                parent[x] = r
            return parent[x], xr[x]

        def unite(u,v,w):
            nonlocal comps, ok
            ru, xu = find(u)
            rv, xv = find(v)
            if ru == rv:
                if (xu ^ xv) != w:
                    ok = False
                return
            if size[ru] < size[rv]:
                parent[ru] = rv
                xr[ru] = w ^ xu ^ xv
                size[rv] += size[ru]
            else:
                parent[rv] = ru
                xr[rv] = w ^ xu ^ xv
                size[ru] += size[rv]
            comps -= 1

        col_pref = [0]*W
        for i,s in enumerate(grid):
            pref = 0
            # process columns
            # Need a0 for j=0 and Q for row0.
            # We'll iterate with index j to know j==0 and update pref after j>=1.
            for j,ch in enumerate(s):
                if ch == 65:
                    a = 1
                else:
                    a = 0
                if j == 0:
                    P = row_par[i] ^ a  # since row_par valid 0, P=a
                else:
                    P = pref
                if a == 0: # B cell
                    if i == 0:
                        # Q = a0 for this column = 1 if s[0] == 'A' else 0
                        # But s is current row; for row0 only. Need a0 at column j.
                        # We can compute Q = 1 if grid[0][j] == 65 else 0. Since i==0, ch is a0.
                        Q = a  # Wait a is a_{i,j}; for i=0, a0. But for B cell a=0, so Q=0? Hold on! For row0 B cell, Q = a_{0,j} (the tile type at (0,j)). But if the cell is B, a=0, so Q=0! Is that correct? Let's check: Q_top for row0 = a_{0,j} due column parity. If cell (0,j) is B (a=0), then Q=0. Yes! Earlier sample row0 B at (0,2), a0=0, Q=0. Good. So for i==0, Q = a (which is 0 for B, 1 for A but we only process B so Q=0). Wait for B cell at row0, a=0, so Q=0. That seems surprising: top edge y_{H-1,j} = c_j ⊕ a0; if a0=0, Q=0. Yes. So Q = a for row0. Since B cell a=0, Q=0. But if we used a0 generally, for B cell a0=0. So Q=0. Good.
                    else:
                        Q = col_pref[j]
                    K = 1 ^ P ^ Q
                    unite(i, H + j, K)
                    if not ok:
                        # can break? We'll break out loops maybe.
                        pass
                if j >= 1 and a:
                    pref ^= 1
            # after processing row i, update col_pref if i>=1
            if i >= 1:
                # update col_pref with row i (a values)
                for j,ch in enumerate(s):
                    if ch == 65:
                        col_pref[j] ^= 1
            if not ok:
                break
        if not ok:
            out.append("0")
        else:
            out.append(str(pow(2, comps, MOD)))
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

Now, DSU find recursion with nested function and `nonlocal`? In Python, `nonlocal comps, ok` inside unite. find doesn't need nonlocal because mutating lists. Need define `sys.setrecursionlimit` at top.

Let's write DSU find carefully:

def find(x):
    if parent[x] != x:
        r, px = find(parent[x])
        xr[x] ^= px
        parent[x] = r
    return parent[x], xr[x]

Suppose parent[x] = p, xr[x] = value[x] xor value[p]. find(p) returns (root, value[p] xor value[root]). Then xr[x] ^= px gives value[x] xor value[p] xor value[p] xor value[root] = value[x] xor value[root]. parent[x]=root. Return root, xr[x]. Good.

Union:
ru, xu = find(u); rv, xv = find(v). xu = value[u] xor value[ru], xv = value[v] xor value[rv].
If ru==rv: value[u] xor value[v] = xu xor xv (since value[ru] cancels). Check == w.
If attach ru under rv:
Need xr[ru] = value[ru] xor value[rv].
We have value[u] xor value[v] = (xu xor value[ru]) xor (xv xor value[rv]) = xu xor xv xor value[ru] xor value[rv] = w.
Thus value[ru] xor value[rv] = w xor xu xor xv. So xr[ru] = w ^ xu ^ xv.
If attach rv under ru:
Need xr[rv] = value[rv] xor value[ru] = value[ru] xor value[rv] = same. So same. Good.

But wait if we attach rv under ru, parent[rv]=ru, xr[rv] = value[rv] xor value[ru] = w ^ xu ^ xv. Yes.

Union by size: if size[ru] < size[rv], attach ru to rv; else attach rv to ru. If equal, attach rv to ru. Fine.

Now, comps decrement only if different roots. If conflict, ok=False but we still may continue? We can break. If break early, no need process remaining. But if we break out of row loop, we still have grid and DSU; output 0. We can implement a flag and break inner loops. However, if we break inner loop, we still need to skip rest of rows? We can just process all rows but if ok false, unite will still be called? We can check `if not ok: return 0` inside processing? But returning from solve_case function is fine. Since we are in main loop, we can define solve_case function to return answer. That may be cleaner and allow early return. But function call per case OK. Let's define `solve_case(H,W,grid)` returning int. Then inside if parity fail return 0. DSU nested. If conflict, return 0 immediately. This avoids processing rest. But if we return from inside nested loops, no need break. We'll define solve_case.

However, defining solve_case outside main and using nested DSU per case is fine. Need pass MOD global.

Let's write solve_case:

def solve_case(H,W,grid):
    col_par = [0]*W
    row_par = [0]*H
    for i,s in enumerate(grid):
        p=0
        for j,ch in enumerate(s):
            if ch == 65:
                p ^= 1
                col_par[j] ^= 1
        row_par[i]=p
    if any(row_par) or any(col_par):
        return 0

    n = H + W
    parent = list(range(n))
    size = [1]*n
    xr = [0]*n
    comps = n

    def find(x):
        if parent[x] != x:
            r, px = find(parent[x])
            xr[x] ^= px
            parent[x] = r
        return parent[x], xr[x]

    def unite(u,v,w):
        nonlocal comps
        ru, xu = find(u)
        rv, xv = find(v)
        if ru == rv:
            return (xu ^ xv) == w
        if size[ru] < size[rv]:
            parent[ru] = rv
            xr[ru] = w ^ xu ^ xv
            size[rv] += size[ru]
        else:
            parent[rv] = ru
            xr[rv] = w ^ xu ^ xv
            size[ru] += size[rv]
        comps -= 1
        return True

    col_pref = [0]*W
    for i,s in enumerate(grid):
        pref = 0
        # local variables for speed
        for j,ch in enumerate(s):
            if ch == 65:
                a = 1
            else:
                a = 0
            if j == 0:
                P = row_par[i] ^ a  # row_par[i] is 0
            else:
                P = pref
            if a == 0:
                if i == 0:
                    Q = 0  # since a=0 for B cell; a_{0,j}=0
                else:
                    Q = col_pref[j]
                K = 1 ^ P ^ Q
                if not unite(i, H + j, K):
                    return 0
            if j >= 1 and a:
                pref ^= 1
        if i >= 1:
            # update col_pref with row i
            # Could combine with previous loop? We already iterated; need second loop over row to update.
            for j,ch in enumerate(s):
                if ch == 65:
                    col_pref[j] ^= 1
    return pow(2, comps, MOD)

def find(x):
    root = x
    acc = 0
    while parent[root] != root:
        acc ^= xr[root]
        root = parent[root]
    # compress path
    while parent[x] != x:
        p = parent[x]
        px = xr[x]
        parent[x] = root
        xr[x] = acc
        acc ^= px
        x = p
    return root, xr_original? Need return xor from original x to root. Let's derive.

We need for original x, after compression, xr[x] should be xor from x to root. We can compute during first loop acc = xor from original x to root? Let's see:
Start node = x. acc=0.
while parent[node] != node:
    acc ^= xr[node]
    node = parent[node]
At end, acc = xr[x] xor xr[parent[x]] xor ... xor xr[last parent] = value[x] xor value[root]. Yes.
Now compress:
cur = x
cur_acc = acc? We need set xr[cur] = xor from cur to root. For cur=x, that's acc. Then move to parent. For next node p (old parent of x), xor from p to root = acc ^ xr[x] (since acc = xr[x] xor xor_p_to_root). So we can maintain `remaining = acc`; for each cur:
    p = parent[cur]
    px = xr[cur]  # xor cur to p
    parent[cur] = root
    xr[cur] = remaining
    remaining ^= px
    cur = p
Let's test: cur=x, remaining=acc. Set xr[x]=acc. remaining ^= px (px=xr[x] old) = acc ^ xr[x] = xor_p_to_root. cur=p. Set xr[p]=remaining. Good. Return root, acc.

But note after compression, if we later call find on a node whose parent was compressed, xr is to root. Good.

Implement iterative find as nested function:
def find(x):
    root = x
    acc = 0
    while parent[root] != root:
        acc ^= xr[root]
        root = parent[root]
    # path compression
    cur = x
    rem = acc
    while parent[cur] != cur:
        p = parent[cur]
        px = xr[cur]
        parent[cur] = root
        xr[cur] = rem
        rem ^= px
        cur = p
    return root, acc

Need be careful: In compression loop, condition `while parent[cur] != cur`; but for nodes already root? If x is root, acc=0, loop not entered. For cur that is root? We stop before processing root. Good. But after setting parent[cur]=root for cur, if cur's parent was root, next cur=p=root, parent[root]==root, loop stops. Good.

However, there is a subtle bug: In compression loop, for cur=x, we set parent[x]=root. Then `rem ^= px` where px = xr[cur] old. But we overwrote xr[cur] before reading px? We should read px before overwriting. In code:
p = parent[cur]
px = xr[cur]
parent[cur] = root
xr[cur] = rem
rem ^= px
cur = p
Good.

This iterative find avoids recursion and is maybe faster. Let's use it.

Union function:
def unite(u,v,w):
    nonlocal comps
    ru, xu = find(u)
    rv, xv = find(v)
    if ru == rv:
        return (xu ^ xv) == w
    if size[ru] < size[rv]:
        parent[ru] = rv
        xr[ru] = w ^ xu ^ xv
        size[rv] += size[ru]
    else:
        parent[rv] = ru
        xr[rv] = w ^ xu ^ xv
        size[ru] += size[rv]
    comps -= 1
    return True

Need ensure `xr[ru] = w ^ xu ^ xv` when attaching ru to rv. Let's re-evaluate with iterative find's `xu` = value[u] xor value[ru], `xv` = value[v] xor value[rv]. Same as before. Good.

Let's write solve_case with iterative find:

def solve_case(H,W,grid):
    col_par = [0]*W
    row_par = [0]*H
    for i,s in enumerate(grid):
        p=0
        for j,ch in enumerate(s):
            if ch == 65:
                p ^= 1
                col_par[j] ^= 1
        row_par[i]=p
    if any(row_par) or any(col_par):
        return 0

    n = H + W
    parent = list(range(n))
    size = [1]*n
    xr = [0]*n
    comps = n

    def find(x):
        root = x
        acc = 0
        while parent[root] != root:
            acc ^= xr[root]
            root = parent[root]
        cur = x
        rem = acc
        while parent[cur] != cur:
            p = parent[cur]
            px = xr[cur]
            parent[cur] = root
            xr[cur] = rem
            rem ^= px
            cur = p
        return root, acc

    def unite(u,v,w):
        nonlocal comps
        ru, xu = find(u)
        rv, xv = find(v)
        if ru == rv:
            return (xu ^ xv) == w
        if size[ru] < size[rv]:
            parent[ru] = rv
            xr[ru] = w ^ xu ^ xv
            size[rv] += size[ru]
        else:
            parent[rv] = ru
            xr[rv] = w ^ xu ^ xv
            size[ru] += size[rv]
        comps -= 1
        return True

    col_pref = [0]*W
    for i,s in enumerate(grid):
        pref = 0
        # localize variables for speed?
        for j,ch in enumerate(s):
            if ch == 65:
                a = 1
            else:
                a = 0
            if j == 0:
                P = row_par[i] ^ a
            else:
                P = pref
            if a == 0:
                if i == 0:
                    Q = 0
                else:
                    Q = col_pref[j]
                K = 1 ^ P ^ Q
                if not unite(i, H + j, K):
                    return 0
            if j >= 1 and a:
                pref ^= 1
        if i >= 1:
            for j,ch in enumerate(s):
                if ch == 65:
                    col_pref[j] ^= 1
    return pow(2, comps, MOD)

K = 1⊕P_i(left)⊕Q_j(top). 1 cancels. Q_j(top) differs: Q_j(top_i) = xor_{k=1}^{i-1} a_{k,j} (for i>0) or a0 for i=0. P_i(left) depends on row. Not obviously zero.

Let's try to find a concrete inconsistency by solving a small system manually. Use H=3,W=4, choose B graph with a 4-cycle and compute K. We can vary A pattern to make K around cycle sum 1.

Let's set B cycle at rows 0,1 and cols 2,3. Need cells (0,2),(0,3),(1,2),(1,3) are B. Thus row0 and row1 have B at 2,3. Row parity even: each row must have even number of A. With W=4, if B at 2,3, the A count in row0 is determined by cols0,1. Could be 0,2,4? Actually row0 A count = a0+a1 (since a2=a3=0). Need even, so a0+a1 even => a0=a1. Similarly row1 a0=a1. Column parity for cols2,3: B at row0,row1, need row2 at col2,3? Column parity even means A count in col2 among rows even. If row0,row1 B (a=0), row2 can be A or B? A count = a2_row2. Need even => row2 B. So row2 col2,3 B. Thus row2 also B at 2,3. For cols0,1, parity depends on a0,a1 rows. Let's choose a0 row0 = a1 row0 = x, row1 same y, row2 maybe z. Column0 parity x⊕y⊕z=0; column1 same. Row2 parity: z0⊕z1? Wait row2 cols0,1 values z0,z1; cols2,3 B. Need row2 parity even => z0⊕z1=0 => z0=z1=z. Column parity x⊕y⊕z=0. So z=x⊕y. Thus pattern parameterized by x,y:
row0: A if x=1 at 0,1; B at2,3.
row1: A if y=1 at0,1; B at2,3.
row2: A if z=x⊕y at0,1; B at2,3.
All valid. Let's compute K for B cycle:
Need P for row0 at j2,j3. Row0 a0=a1=x, a2=a3=0.
P j2 = xor_{k=1}^{1} a_k = a1 = x.
P j3 = xor_{k=1}^{2} a_k = a1⊕a2 = x.
So P row0 col2=col3=x.
Row1 similarly P = y.
Row2 B at2,3: P = a1_row2 = z? For row2 a0=a1=z, so P j2=z, j3=z.
Q for row0 B =0.
Q for row1 = col_pref before row1 =0 (excludes row0).
Q for row2 = col_pref after row1 = row1 values at col2,3 =0 (since B). So Q row2 col2,3=0.
K:
(0,2): 1^x
(0,3): 1^x
(1,2): 1^y
(1,3): 1^y
(2,2): 1^z
(2,3): 1^z
Consider cycle r0-c2-r2-c3-r1-c2? Need edges: r0-c2 (1^x), r2-c2 (1^z), r2-c3 (1^z), r1-c3 (1^y), r1-c2 (1^y), r0? Not a simple cycle. Let's find a simple cycle: r0-c2-r2-c3-r1-c2? ends at c2 not r0. Need r0-c3 edge exists: cycle r0-c2-r2-c3-r0? Wait r0-c3 edge exists. Cycle: r0 - c2 - r2 - c3 - r0. Edges: r0-c2 (1^x), r2-c2 (1^z), r2-c3 (1^z), r0-c3 (1^x). Sum = (1^x)^(1^z)^(1^z)^(1^x) =0. Another cycle r1-c2-r2-c3-r1-c3? r1-c2 (1^y), r2-c2 (1^z), r2-c3 (1^z), r1-c3 (1^y) sum0. So consistent.

What about cycle r0-c2-r1-c2? Not a cycle (two edges). Need 4-cycle with rows0,1 and cols2,3: r0-c2-r1-c3-r0? Need r0-c3 and r1-c2. Sum (1^x)^(1^y)^(1^y)^(1^x)=0. Always 0. So this family consistent.

Try B cycle at rows0,1 and cols1,2 where P values differ between col1 and col2 if a1? Let's parameterize B at (0,1),(0,2),(1,1),(1,2). Row0 cols1,2 B, so row0 A count = a0+a3 (since a1=a2=0) must be even => a0=a3. Row1 similarly a0=a3. Column parity for col1,2: row0,row1 B, row2 must B. Row2 cols1,2 B, row2 parity even => row2 a0=a3. Let x=a0 row0=a3 row0, y=a0 row1=a3 row1, z=a0 row2=a3 row2, with column parity col0: x⊕y⊕z=0, col3 same. So z=x⊕y.
Compute P:
Row0: a0=x,a1=0,a2=0,a3=x.
P j1 (left of col1) = xor_{k=1}^{0}=0.
P j2 = a1=0.
So K row0 col1=1, col2=1.
Row1 same K=1.
Row2: a0=z,a1=0,a2=0,a3=z. P j1=0,j2=0. Q row2 col1,2 = col_pref after row1 = row1 col1,2 =0. K=1.
All K=1, consistent.

Try B cycle at rows0,1 and cols0,1. Row0 cols0,1 B, row0 A count = a2+a3 even => a2=a3. Similar, K all? P for col0 = a0? B at col0 P=a0=0. col1 P=0. K=1. Consistent.

It seems K around cycles always cancels. Maybe consistency is guaranteed. But DSU still fine.

Now, let's consider if we can simplify answer to 2^{components} without conflict; but DSU conflict check not harmful. However, if consistency always guaranteed, DSU conflict never triggers. But if due to a bug in K formula, conflict might trigger incorrectly. We trust formula.

Now, let's think about the possibility of multiple connected components and answer 2^{components}. Suppose B graph has a component with no edges (isolated vertex). It contributes factor 2. But does an isolated row variable r_i appear in any constraint? It appears in E_x for that row but no B relation. It is free. Yes.

Now, let's consider if there are constraints from A cells that couple r and c indirectly through x,y? A cells impose x flip and y flip, already solved. No coupling. Good.

Now, let's think about the row/column parity conditions in terms of A count. We compute col_par by XOR of A. If a row has odd A, output 0. But what if H or W even/odd? Row parity condition is necessary regardless. For all A, row parity = W mod2. So if W odd, all A grid has no solution. Is that true? Let's test H=2,W=3 all A. Each cell Type A. Can we orient to no dead ends? Our equations: row parity W=3 odd -> no solution. Is that plausible? Let's try to reason: In a row of all A, x must alternate around cycle. An odd cycle cannot alternate, so impossible. Yes.

Similarly all A H=3,W=2: column parity H=3 odd -> no solution. Good.

Now, let's think about if row parity even and column parity even are also sufficient for E_x/E_y but not for B. We handle.

Let's write final code:

import sys
MOD = 998244353

def solve_case(H,W,grid):
    col_par = [0]*W
    row_par = [0]*H
    for i,s in enumerate(grid):
        p=0
        for j,ch in enumerate(s):
            if ch == 65:
                p ^= 1
                col_par[j] ^= 1
        row_par[i]=p
    if any(row_par) or any(col_par):
        return 0

    n = H + W
    parent = list(range(n))
    size = [1]*n
    xr = [0]*n
    comps = n

    def find(x):
        root = x
        acc = 0
        while parent[root] != root:
            acc ^= xr[root]
            root = parent[root]
        cur = x
        rem = acc
        while parent[cur] != cur:
            p = parent[cur]
            px = xr[cur]
            parent[cur] = root
            xr[cur] = rem
            rem ^= px
            cur = p
        return root, acc

    def unite(u,v,w):
        nonlocal comps
        ru, xu = find(u)
        rv, xv = find(v)
        if ru == rv:
            return (xu ^ xv) == w
        if size[ru] < size[rv]:
            parent[ru] = rv
            xr[ru] = w ^ xu ^ xv
            size[rv] += size[ru]
        else:
            parent[rv] = ru
            xr[rv] = w ^ xu ^ xv
            size[ru] += size[rv]
        comps -= 1
        return True

    col_pref = [0]*W
    for i,s in enumerate(grid):
        pref = 0
        # process row
        for j,ch in enumerate(s):
            if ch == 65:
                a = 1
            else:
                a = 0
            if j == 0:
                P = row_par[i] ^ a
            else:
                P = pref
            if a == 0:
                if i == 0:
                    Q = 0
                else:
                    Q = col_pref[j]
                K = 1 ^ P ^ Q
                if not unite(i, H + j, K):
                    return 0
            if j >= 1 and a:
                pref ^= 1
        if i >= 1:
            for j,ch in enumerate(s):
                if ch == 65:
                    col_pref[j] ^= 1
    return pow(2, comps, MOD)

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    it = iter(data)
    T = int(next(it))
    out = []
    for _ in range(T):
        H = int(next(it)); W = int(next(it))
        grid = [next(it) for _ in range(H)]
        out.append(str(solve_case(H,W,grid)))
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

We store xr[x] = value[x] XOR value[parent[x]].
find returns (root, xr_to_root) where xr_to_root = value[x] XOR value[root].
For edge u,v with relation value[u] XOR value[v] = w.
find u: xu = value[u] XOR value[ru].
find v: xv = value[v] XOR value[rv].
If ru != rv, we want set parent[ru]=rv and xr[ru] = value[ru] XOR value[rv].
From relation:
value[u] XOR value[v] = w
=> (xu XOR value[ru]) XOR (xv XOR value[rv]) = w
=> value[ru] XOR value[rv] = w XOR xu XOR xv.
So xr[ru] = w ^ xu ^ xv. If attach ru to rv. If attach rv to ru, we need xr[rv] = value[rv] XOR value[ru] = value[ru] XOR value[rv] (same in GF2) = w ^ xu ^ xv. So same. Good.

def solve_case(H,W,grid,row_par,col_par):
    if any(row_par) or any(col_par):
        return 0
    n = H + W
    parent = list(range(n))
    size = [1]*n
    xr = [0]*n
    comps = n

    def find(x): ...
    def unite(u,v,w): ...

    col_pref = [0]*W
    for i,s in enumerate(grid):
        pref=0
        for j,ch in enumerate(s):
            if ch == 65:
                a=1
            else:
                a=0
            if j == 0:
                P = row_par[i] ^ a
            else:
                P = pref
            if a == 0:
                if i == 0:
                    Q = 0
                else:
                    Q = col_pref[j]
                K = 1 ^ P ^ Q
                if not unite(i, H+j, K):
                    return 0
            if j >= 1 and a:
                pref ^= 1
        if i >= 1:
            for j,ch in enumerate(s):
                if ch == 65:
                    col_pref[j] ^= 1
    return pow(2, comps, MOD)

Main:
def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    T = int(input())
    out=[]
    for _ in range(T):
        H,W = map(int,input().split())
        grid=[]
        row_par=[0]*H
        col_par=[0]*W
        for i in range(H):
            s = input().strip()
            grid.append(s)
            p=0
            for j,ch in enumerate(s):
                if ch == 65:
                    p ^= 1
                    col_par[j] ^= 1
            row_par[i]=p
        out.append(str(solve_case(H,W,grid,row_par,col_par)))
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

def solve_case(H,W,grid,row_par,col_par):
    if any(row_par) or any(col_par):
        return 0
    n = H + W
    parent = list(range(n))
    size = [1]*n
    xr = [0]*n
    comps = n
    ...
    col_pref = [0]*W
    for i in range(H):
        base = i * W
        pref = 0
        for j in range(W):
            ch = grid[base + j]
            if ch == 65:
                a = 1
            else:
                a = 0
            if j == 0:
                P = row_par[i] ^ a
            else:
                P = pref
            if a == 0:
                if i == 0:
                    Q = 0
                else:
                    Q = col_pref[j]
                K = 1 ^ P ^ Q
                if not unite(i, H + j, K):
                    return 0
            if j >= 1 and a:
                pref ^= 1
        if i >= 1:
            for j in range(W):
                if grid[base + j] == 65:
                    col_pref[j] ^= 1
    return pow(2, comps, MOD)

    grid.extend(s)
    p=0
    for j,ch in enumerate(s):
        if ch == 65:
            p ^= 1
            col_par[j] ^= 1
    row_par[i]=p

Before final, let's maybe think about a proof of the linear system to ensure no missing factor. Let's derive from tile types one more time.

Let L,R,U,D be bits. For A: segment connects adjacent edges. Adjacent pairs are exactly one horizontal (L/R) and one vertical (U/D). So L⊕R=1, U⊕D=1. For B: segment connects opposite edges. Either horizontal: L=R=1,U=D=0; or vertical: L=R=0,U=D=1. This is equivalent to L=R, U=D, L⊕U=1. Good.

Set a=1 for A, 0 for B. Then for all cells:
R = L ⊕ a (A flips, B copies).
D = U ⊕ a (A flips, B copies).
For B, L⊕U=1.

Now, define r_i = x_{i,0}. For row i, R = x_{i,j}, L=x_{i,j-1}. Recurrence x_{i,j}=x_{i,j-1}⊕a_{i,j}. Sum over j: 0 = ⊕ a_{i,j}, so row parity even. Then x_{i,j}=r_i⊕P_i(j) with P_i(0)=0, P_i(j)=⊕_{k=1}^j a_{i,k} for j>=1, and P_i(W-1)=a_{i,0}. For B at (i,j), L=x_{i,j-1}=r_i⊕P_i(j-1 mod W). We compute P accordingly.

Similarly c_j = y_{0,j}; y_{i,j}=c_j⊕Q_j(i) with Q_j(0)=0, Q_j(i)=⊕_{k=1}^i a_{k,j}, Q_j(H-1)=a_{0,j}. For B at (i,j), U=y_{i-1,j}=c_j⊕Q_j(i-1 mod H). We compute Q accordingly.

Then B equation L⊕U=1 gives r_i⊕c_j = 1⊕P⊕Q. Good.

Now, row0 Q=0 for B because a0=0. But in formula Q_j(i-1 mod H) for i=0 is Q_j(H-1)=a_{0,j}. Since B cell a0=0, Q=0. Good.

Now, P for j=0 is P_i(W-1)=a_{i,0}; B cell a0=0, P=0. Our code P=row_par[i]^a =0. Good.

Now, if row_par valid, P for j=0 = a0. If a0=0, P=0. Good.

Now, if col_par valid, Q for i=0 = a0. If a0=0, Q=0. Good.

Now, all consistent.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the fact that for B cell at i>0, Q=col_pref[j] = xor_{k=1}^{i-1} a_{k,j}. This is Q_j(i-1). Yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the fact that for B cell at i>0, col_pref before processing row i is xor_{k=1}^{i-1} a_k. We update after row i-1. Let's prove by induction:
Before row1, col_pref=0 = xor_{k=1}^{0}.
After row1, col_pref ^= row1 = xor_{k=1}^{1}.
Before row2, col_pref = xor_{k=1}^{1}. Good.
So before row i, col_pref = xor_{k=1}^{i-1}. Good.

def find(x, parent=parent, xr=xr):
    root = x
    acc = 0
    while parent[root] != root:
        acc ^= xr[root]
        root = parent[root]
    cur = x
    rem = acc
    while parent[cur] != cur:
        p = parent[cur]
        px = xr[cur]
        parent[cur] = root
        xr[cur] = rem
        rem ^= px
        cur = p
    return root, acc

def unite(u, v, w, parent=parent, size=size, xr=xr):
    nonlocal comps
    ru, xu = find(u)
    rv, xv = find(v)
    ...
But `find` is a closure; unite calls find. Could bind `find` local. Fine.

Let's maybe write the final code cleanly:

import sys
MOD = 998244353

def solve_case(H, W, grid, row_par, col_par):
    if any(row_par) or any(col_par):
        return 0

    n = H + W
    parent = list(range(n))
    size = [1] * n
    xr = [0] * n
    comps = n

    def find(x, parent=parent, xr=xr):
        root = x
        acc = 0
        while parent[root] != root:
            acc ^= xr[root]
            root = parent[root]
        cur = x
        rem = acc
        while parent[cur] != cur:
            p = parent[cur]
            px = xr[cur]
            parent[cur] = root
            xr[cur] = rem
            rem ^= px
            cur = p
        return root, acc

    def unite(u, v, w, parent=parent, size=size, xr=xr):
        nonlocal comps
        ru, xu = find(u)
        rv, xv = find(v)
        if ru == rv:
            return (xu ^ xv) == w
        if size[ru] < size[rv]:
            parent[ru] = rv
            xr[ru] = w ^ xu ^ xv
            size[rv] += size[ru]
        else:
            parent[rv] = ru
            xr[rv] = w ^ xu ^ xv
            size[ru] += size[rv]
        comps -= 1
        return True

    col_pref = [0] * W
    for i in range(H):
        base = i * W
        pref = 0
        for j in range(W):
            ch = grid[base + j]
            if ch == 65:
                a = 1
            else:
                a = 0

            if j == 0:
                P = row_par[i] ^ a
            else:
                P = pref

            if a == 0:
                if i == 0:
                    Q = 0
                else:
                    Q = col_pref[j]
                K = 1 ^ P ^ Q
                if not unite(i, H + j, K):
                    return 0

            if j >= 1 and a:
                pref ^= 1

        if i >= 1:
            for j in range(W):
                if grid[base + j] == 65:
                    col_pref[j] ^= 1

    return pow(2, comps, MOD)

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    T = int(input())
    out = []
    for _ in range(T):
        H, W = map(int, input().split())
        grid = bytearray()
        row_par = [0] * H
        col_par = [0] * W
        for i in range(H):
            s = input().strip()
            grid.extend(s)
            p = 0
            for j, ch in enumerate(s):
                if ch == 65:
                    p ^= 1
                    col_par[j] ^= 1
            row_par[i] = p
        out.append(str(solve_case(H, W, grid, row_par, col_par)))
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

MOD = 998244353


def solve_case(H, W, grid, row_par, col_par):
    if any(row_par) or any(col_par):
        return 0

    n = H + W
    parent = list(range(n))
    size = [1] * n
    xr = [0] * n
    comps = n

    def find(x, parent=parent, xr=xr):
        root = x
        acc = 0
        while parent[root] != root:
            acc ^= xr[root]
            root = parent[root]

        cur = x
        rem = acc
        while parent[cur] != cur:
            p = parent[cur]
            px = xr[cur]
            parent[cur] = root
            xr[cur] = rem
            rem ^= px
            cur = p

        return root, acc

    def unite(u, v, w, parent=parent, size=size, xr=xr):
        nonlocal comps
        ru, xu = find(u)
        rv, xv = find(v)

        if ru == rv:
            return (xu ^ xv) == w

        if size[ru] < size[rv]:
            parent[ru] = rv
            xr[ru] = w ^ xu ^ xv
            size[rv] += size[ru]
        else:
            parent[rv] = ru
            xr[rv] = w ^ xu ^ xv
            size[ru] += size[rv]

        comps -= 1
        return True

    col_pref = [0] * W

    for i in range(H):
        base = i * W
        pref = 0

        for j in range(W):
            ch = grid[base + j]
            a = 1 if ch == 65 else 0

            if j == 0:
                P = row_par[i] ^ a
            else:
                P = pref

            if a == 0:
                if i == 0:
                    Q = 0
                else:
                    Q = col_pref[j]

                K = 1 ^ P ^ Q
                if not unite(i, H + j, K):
                    return 0

            if j >= 1 and a:
                pref ^= 1

        if i >= 1:
            for j in range(W):
                if grid[base + j] == 65:
                    col_pref[j] ^= 1

    return pow(2, comps, MOD)


def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    T = int(input())
    ans = []

    for _ in range(T):
        H, W = map(int, input().split())

        grid = bytearray()
        row_par = [0] * H
        col_par = [0] * W

        for i in range(H):
            s = input().strip()
            grid.extend(s)

            p = 0
            for j, ch in enumerate(s):
                if ch == 65:  # 'A'
                    p ^= 1
                    col_par[j] ^= 1

            row_par[i] = p

        ans.append(str(solve_case(H, W, grid, row_par, col_par)))

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(ans))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
